log above), so differences near zero are not exaggerated.

### 06_length_vs_engagement.png - Engagement vs word count
WHAT IT SHOWS: each dot is a text work (log10 word count vs kudos/hits). Thick lines are the
median per word-count bin; both categories share the same bin edges, so they are compared at
the same lengths. Faded markers = bins with fewer than 5 works.
NUMBERS:
  F/F: n=1175, median words 1,717, Spearman rho(length, kudos/hits)=-0.08 (p=0.00779) -> longer works have lower kudos/hits
  M/M: n=8124, median words 3,056, Spearman rho(length, kudos/hits)=-0.24 (p=1.12e-108) -> longer works have lower kudos/hits
CAUTION: if the two categories have different length distributions, a raw category gap
may partly be a length effect. Compare the lines at the same x position.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom and metric, whether M/M and F/F distributions differ
(Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across all tests run). Cliff's delta is the
effect size: -1 to +1, positive = M/M values tend to be higher; roughly |d|<0.15 negligible,
<0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

### 01_engagement_ecdfs.png - Engagement distributions (ECDF)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom (row) and ratio (column), the share of works at or below
each value, for F/F, M/M, F/M and all works (dashed grey). A curve further RIGHT = generally
higher engagement; a curve that rises quickly on the left = many low-engagement works. The
x-axis is symlog (linear below 1, log above), so differences near zero are not exaggerated.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

CAUTION: if the groups have different length distributions, a raw category gap may partly
be a length effect. Compare the lines at the same x position.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

be a length effect. Compare the lines at the same x position.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Works tagged with BOTH categories of a pair are left out of that pair's test so the two groups
share no works (the number left out is shown as 'overlap excluded').
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

figures (green). A work that lists two categories counts in both, so shares add to more than 100%.

found by raking: each work's weight is adjusted until the weighted share of works listing each

### 06_length_vs_engagement.png - Engagement vs word count

Thick lines are the median per word-count bin; all groups, including the dashed 'all works'

with fewer than 5 works.

  F/F: n=9222, median words 3,688, Spearman rho(length, kudos/hits)=-0.30 (p=4.79e-196) -> longer works have lower kudos/hits
  M/M: n=26444, median words 3,326, Spearman rho(length, kudos/hits)=-0.22 (p=2.72e-291) -> longer works have lower kudos/hits
  F/M: n=26971, median words 3,656, Spearman rho(length, kudos/hits)=-0.22 (p=1.8e-289) -> longer works have lower kudos/hits
  All works (weighted): n=63667, median words 2,587, Spearman rho(length, kudos/hits)=-0.18 -> longer works have lower kudos/hits
CAUTION: if the groups have different length distributions, a raw category gap may partly
be a length effect. Compare the lines at the same x position.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Works tagged with BOTH categories of a pair are left out of that pair's test so the two groups
share no works (the number left out is shown as 'overlap excluded').
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.

higher engagement; a curve that rises quickly on the left = many low-engagement works. The
x-axis is symlog (linear below 1, log above), so differences near zero are not exaggerated.

### 05_warnings_proxy.png - Kudos/hits by intense-content warning

with fewer than 5 works.

CAUTION: if the groups have different length distributions, a raw category gap may partly
be a length effect. Compare the lines at the same x position.

### Statistical tests (printed to terminal; not a figure)
WHAT IT SHOWS: for each fandom, pair of categories and metric, whether the two distributions
differ (Mann-Whitney U, two-sided; Benjamini-Hochberg FDR across ALL tests run). Pairs tested:
  M/M vs F/F; M/M vs F/M; F/F vs F/M. 'All works' is not tested (it contains the others).
Works tagged with BOTH categories of a pair are left out of that pair's test so the two groups
share no works (the number left out is shown as 'overlap excluded').
Cliff's delta is the effect size: -1 to +1, positive = the FIRST group's values tend to be higher;
roughly |d|<0.15 negligible, <0.33 small, <0.47 medium, otherwise large. ** = FDR-adjusted p < 0.05.
Groups need n >= 15 in BOTH categories or the test is skipped.
